▶ position manager: structural and broker protection
  ✔ structural SL: a CONFIRMED close beyond the stop exits at that close (thesis invalidation), before the broker SL (1.5403ms)
  ✔ broker SL: an intrabar spike through 1.5 x structural + spread closes at exactly that distance plus slippage and is never widened (0.4316ms)
  ✔ thesis deterioration (3 adverse closes, beyond half R and the entry bar) exits early with a smaller loss than invalidation (0.6408ms)
  ✔ SELL mirror: spread is paid on the exit side and the structural stop is above the fill (0.6026ms)
✔ position manager: structural and broker protection (3.6256ms)
▶ position manager: HOLD / PROFIT_AVAILABLE / PROTECT / HARVEST / EXIT
  ✔ profit milestone detection: PROFIT_AVAILABLE at the first close with MFE >= trigger R (0.75 R for D1, 1.0 R for D2) (0.9773ms)
  ✔ HOLD: with no profit available and no deterioration the manager holds and leaves protection unchanged (0.3605ms)
  ✔ HARVEST on weak continuation: a spike to the trigger followed by a weak close banks at that close (0.405ms)
  ✔ PROTECT + RUN on strong continuation: floor activates from the next bar, ratchets upward, and the floor touch banks the floor (0.4765ms)
  ✔ EXIT on adverse break during a protected run (harvest at the close, never below the floor) (0.3977ms)
  ✔ HARVEST when the remaining reward compresses (distance to TP2 < 0.5 ATR) (0.216ms)
  ✔ simple bank (CH-B) closes at the first close after 1 R (0.1593ms)
  ✔ production-like (CH-A) closes on retrace >= 0.5 of the best excursion after 1 R, or at the +30 USD monetary target (0.4959ms)
✔ position manager: HOLD / PROFIT_AVAILABLE / PROTECT / HARVEST / EXIT (3.9266ms)

▶ cdpLock: tryAcquireCdpLock() basic acquire/release
  ✔ acquires a free lock, writing a pid+timestamp lock file (8.5359ms)
  ✔ a second acquisition attempt fails while a live, fresh holder exists (simulated as a different pid) (5.1879ms)
  ✔ releaseCdpLock only removes a lock this SAME process holds (7.4335ms)
  ✔ releaseCdpLock never removes another process's lock, even a stale one it did not itself reclaim (6.6083ms)
  ✔ releaseCdpLock never throws even if the lock file is already gone (2.0583ms)
✔ cdpLock: tryAcquireCdpLock() basic acquire/release (32.1672ms)
▶ cdpLock: stale-lock reclaim
  ✔ reclaims a lock whose holder process is no longer alive (7.2755ms)
  ✔ reclaims a lock older than maxAgeMs even if the holder PID still reports alive ("no permanent stale lock") (8.4981ms)
  ✔ does NOT reclaim a fresh lock from a still-alive holder (6.8816ms)
  ✔ treats a corrupt/torn lock file as absent (fails open to acquisition, never crashes) (6.4975ms)
✔ cdpLock: stale-lock reclaim (30.071ms)
▶ cdpLock: re-entrancy -- fails fast, never deadlocks
  ✔ tryAcquireCdpLock throws CDP_LOCK_REENTRANT if the CURRENT process already holds the lock (6.8071ms)
  ✔ withCdpLock rejects with CDP_LOCK_REENTRANT (not a hang) if fn() tries to re-acquire the SAME lock nested inside itself (8.4948ms)
✔ cdpLock: re-entrancy -- fails fast, never deadlocks (15.7581ms)
▶ cdpLock: acquireCdpLock() polling + timeout
  ✔ polls until a busy lock frees up, then acquires it (proves serialization: a second "operation" WAITS its turn rather than racing) (35.8049ms)
  ✔ throws CDP_LOCK_TIMEOUT rather than hanging forever if the lock never frees up (43.9179ms)
✔ cdpLock: acquireCdpLock() polling + timeout (80.5277ms)
▶ cdpLock: withCdpLock() always releases
  ✔ releases after fn() resolves successfully (6.0438ms)
  ✔ releases after fn() throws (a WAIT-shaped/error result never leaves the lock held) (5.699ms)
  ✔ two sequential withCdpLock() calls on the SAME path never see each other's lock (serialized, not deadlocked) (7.7468ms)
  ✔ proves cross-process mutual exclusion via a simulated different-pid holder: a competing acquisition never wins while the (other-process) holder is live and fresh, and wins immediately once it is not (14.4237ms)
  ✔ within the SAME process, a second (non-nested but concurrent) withCdpLock() call on the SAME path fails fast with CDP_LOCK_REENTRANT rather than hanging or double-entering fn() (42.1207ms)
  ✔ leaves no leftover .tmp- files behind after normal sequential use (Windows-race hardening cleans up its own losing temp files) (44.1743ms)
✔ cdpLock: withCdpLock() always releases (121.0866ms)

▶ Born-consumed: CLOSE vs WICK, computed and wired separately

  ✔ pivot HIGH: a gap bar whose CLOSE exceeds the pivot → both wick-interacted and close-consumed true (close implies wick) (0.4328ms)

▶ P4B runtime-safety: every array.size()-bounded for-loop is guarded against an empty array
  ✔ every "for i = 0 to array.size(IDENT) - 1" is immediately preceded (skipping blank/comment lines) by "if array.size(IDENT) > 0" (3.2676ms)
  ✔ the P5 OUTCOME RESOLUTION backward loop (for safe in-loop array.remove) is guarded against an empty openSignals array exactly like every forward loop (1.4842ms)
  ✔ array.remove() now legitimately exists (P5 review/fix), but ONLY as the one safe in-loop removal inside the guarded, backward-iterating P5 resolution loop — no other unsafe indexed-collection function (array.first/array.last/array.pop/array.insert) exists anywhere (2.1744ms)
  ✔ array.shift() call sites remain safe: P2's two capacity-eviction sites remain guarded by "size > maxActiveLevels" preceded by array.push() (guaranteeing size >= 1); P5's recentHistory (closed-only) eviction is guarded by "size >= p5MaxTrackedSignals" instead (a capacity check ahead of the push, not a post-push overflow check — still provably safe, since array.shift() on an empty array is never reachable there either: the guard only ever removes an EXISTING oldest element to make room, and array.push() unconditionally follows it in the same block). Critically, openSignals (the never-evicted OPEN tracker) must have NO array.shift() call anywhere. (3.371ms)
  ✔ the debug-table convenience reads (last-level consumed/swept) remain guarded by their own ternary size>0 check — unchanged by this patch (1.1226ms)
  ✔ f_nearestAbove and f_nearestBelow (SL/TP2 structural-anchor helpers) are also guarded (0.6584ms)
